## \|\| 1.2.8 \|\|

*sambhoga-praptir iti cen na vaisesyat \|\| 1.2.8 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that the Supreme Lord within the body would also have the same experience as the living entity, we say that this is not true, for He has distinguished attributes.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If the Supreme Lord is considered all-pervading, then He would experience pain and pleasure along with the embodied beings." It is not so, for He is distinguished by His supreme power.

## \|\| 1.2.9 \|\|

*atta caracara-grahanat \|\| 1.2.9 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is the devourer, for He swallows all moving and non-moving beings.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord is described in the scriptures as the devourer (atta) of all elements, for He swallows all moving and non-moving beings.

## \|\| 1.2.10 \|\|

*prakaranac ca \|\| 1.2.10 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the topic is about Him.**

**Комментарий:**

And because the Supreme Lord is the topic in that passage.

## \|\| 1.2.11 \|\|

*guham pravistav atmanau hi tad-darsanat \|\| 1.2.11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Soul and the individual soul have entered within the heart, for this is seen in the scriptures.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord and the living entity are the two souls situated within the heart, and not the intelligence and the living entity, for just as it is seen in this world that two or more things of the same kind are grouped together, so is in the other world.

## \|\| 1.2.12 \|\|

*visesanac ca \|\| 1.2.12 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And also because of their different attributes.**

**Комментарий:**

The living entity and the Supreme Lord are also considered the two souls in the heart because they are described by different attributes as the meditator and the object of meditation.

## \|\| 1.2.13 \|\|

*antara upapatteh \|\| 1.2.13 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Soul is the person in the eye, as there is evidence for this.**

**Комментарий:**

The person in the eye is the Supreme Lord Visnu, for He is endowed with all the mentioned qualities such as immortality.

## \|\| 1.2.14 \|\|

*sthanadi-vyapadesac ca \|\| 1.2.14 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because His places and other descriptions are mentioned.**

**Комментарий:**

And because it is stated in the sruti that the Supreme Soul is situated in places like the eye and so on.

## \|\| 1.2.15 \|\|

*sukha-visistabhidhanad evaca \|\| 1.2.15 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And also because of the description that the Supreme Lord is characterized by blissfulness.**

**Комментарий:**

And because it is mentioned that the Supreme Lord is distinguished by blissfulness.

## \|\| 1.2.16 \|\|

*srutopanisatka-gaty-abhidhanac ca \|\| 1.2.16 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because of the description of the path of one who has heard the Upanisads.**

**Комментарий:**

And because one who has heard the Upanisads and one who knows the person in the eye go through the same path.

## \|\| 1.2.17 \|\|

*anavasthiter asambhavac ca netarah \|\| 1.2.17 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**No one besides the Supreme Lord can be the person in the eye, for a jiva cannot be always situated there and because it cannot possibly have the described attributes.**

**Комментарий:**

The person in the eye cannot be considered a reflection, for a reflection cannot be always situated there, nor can it be the jiva, for the jiva is not always endowed with attributes such as immortality.

## \|\| 1.2.18 \|\|

*antaryamy adhidaivadisu tad-dharma-vyapadesat \|\| 1.2.18 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Soul is the inner controller among all demigods and material elements, for His attributes are mentioned.**

**Комментарий:**

Lord Visnu is indicated as the Supreme Soul within all demigods and other beings, all material elements and everything that exists, for the scriptures teach that He has attributes such as supreme control over the earth and all planetary systems.

## \|\| 1.2.19 \|\|

*na ca smartam atad-dharmabhilapat \|\| 1.2.19 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Pradhana cannot be the inner ruler, for its attributes are stated to be different.**

**Комментарий:**

The pradhana described in the Samkhya smrti cannot be considered the inner ruler, for there is no mention of its possessing such attributes.

## \|\| 1.2.20 \|\|

*sariras cobhaye’pi hi bhedenainam adhiyate \|\| 1.2.20 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The embodied living entity also cannot be the inner ruler because both the Kanvas and the Madhyandinas teach that the jiva and the Supreme Soul are different.**

**Комментарий:**

A living entity also cannot be said to be the inner ruler here, for the knowers of the truth in both the Kanva and the Madhyandina branches declare that the jiva is different from the Supreme Lord.

## \|\| 1.2.21 \|\|

*adrsyatvadi-gunako dharmokteh \|\| 1.2.21 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because of the mention of His attributes, the Supreme Lord is the one Who possesses the qualities of invisibility and so on.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord is the possessor of qualities such as invisibility, for these are mentioned as His attributes.

## \|\| 1.2.22 \|\|

*visesana-bheda-vyapadesabhyam ca netarau \|\| 1.2.22 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By the statement of the Lord’s attributes and difference, neither prakrti nor the jiva can be meant here.**

**Комментарий:**

Only the Supreme Lord is meant in those statements of the Upanisad, and not prakrti or the jiva, for it is taught therein that He is different from both, being distinguished by His attributes such as divine nature.

## \|\| 1.2.23 \|\|

*rupopanyasac ca \|\| 1.2.23 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because of the statement about His form.**

**Комментарий:**

Only the Supreme Soul has the quality of being the original form of everything, for in the Upanisad it is written so.

## \|\| 1.2.24 \|\|

*prakaranac ca \|\| 1.2.24 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the Supreme Soul is the topic.**

**Комментарий:**

And also because the Supreme Lord is the topic in that passage. There is no doubt about this.

## \|\| 1.2.25 \|\|

*vaisvanarah sadharana-sabda-visesat \|\| 1.2.25 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is Vaisvanara, for this word is used in a sense different from the ordinary one.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord, Who is of the size of a short span, is the one called ‘Vaisvanara ’ in the sruti because of His distinguished attributes such as having the heavens as His head.

## \|\| 1.2.26 \|\|

*smaryamanam anumanam syad iti \|\| 1.2.26 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is also ascertained by inferring from the smrti.**

**Комментарий:**

It is concluded that this passage of the Upanisad describes the Supreme Lord, for He is likewise called by such a name in the smrti which conveys the same meaning.

## \|\| 1.2.27 \|\|

*sabdadibhyo’ntah-pratisthanac ca neti cen na*  
*tatha drsty-upadesad asambhavat purusam api cainam adhiyate \|\| 1.2.27 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that on account of the vocabulary, description, and its presence within the body, fire must be meant here and not the Supreme Lord, we say that this is not true because such descriptions are meant to teach meditation on the Supreme Lord, because they cannot possibly apply to the fire in the stomach, and because the sruti teaches about Him in the form of fire.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “According to my view, because of the word ‘agni’ and its presence within the body, here fire is being expressed, and not Lord Visnu. ” It is not so, for this is a description of His worship in the form of fire in the stomach, and because attributes such as having the heavens as His head cannot possibly exist in that fire. The learned scholars of the Vajasaneya branch declare that this Vaisvanara is the Supreme Lord and that the description in the sruti cannot thoroughly apply to the fire in the stomach.

## \|\| 1.2.28 \|\|

*ata eva na devata bhutam ca \|\| 1.2.28 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**For this same reason, the aforementioned fire can be neither the controlling deity of fire nor the fire element.**

**Комментарий:**

And for this same reason, here neither the fire element nor the controlling deity of fire is being described.

## \|\| 1.2.29 \|\|

*saksad apy avirodham jaiminih \|\| 1.2.29 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**According to Jaimini, without any contradiction, Vaisvanara also directly means the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The view of the sage Jaimini regarding the Supreme Lord’s having the size of a short span is stated here. The Supreme Lord's multiple forms are directly He Himself Therefore, although He is all-pervading His minuteness is also proved. So says Jaimini.

## \|\| 1.2.30 \|\|

*abhivyakter ity asmarathyah \|\| 1.2.30 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Asmarathya’s opinion is that the Supreme Lord is manifest there in a formless aspect.**

## \|\| 1.2.31 \|\|

*anusmrter iti badarih \|\| 1.2.31 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Badari’s view is that the Supreme Lord assumes such a size for being meditated upon.**

**Комментарий:**

Asmarathya says that although the Supreme Lord is formless, this form in the size of a short span is possible by His maya, while Badari states that a form in such a dimension is described just for the sake of meditation.

## \|\| 1.2.32 \|\|

*sampatter iti jaiminis tatha hi darsayati \|\| 1.2.32 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**According to Jaimini, this is possible by the Lord’s inconceivable energy, for the scriptures demonstrate so.**

**Комментарий:**

Jaimini says that by dint of His inconceivable energy, the one and the same Supreme Lord becomes many, for this is seen in the sruti and the smrti.

## \|\| 1.2.33 \|\|

*amananti cainam asmin \|\| 1.2.33 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The scriptures declare that the Supreme Lord is the one situated in the size of a short span.**

**Комментарий:**

The conclusion is that the scriptures declare that the Supreme Lord, Who has heaven as His head, is also situated in the size of a short span, for His inconceivable energy is everything and is everywhere.

## Section 3

## \|\| 1.3.1 \|\|

*dyu-bhv-ady-ayatanam sva-sabdat \|\| 1.3.1 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is the abode of heaven, earth and so on, because the word ‘atma’ refers to Him.**

**Комментарий:**

Because of the employment of the word ‘atma, ’ the Supreme Lord is the abode of heaven, earth and so on, and not pradhana.

## \|\| 1.3.2 \|\|

*muktopasrpya-vyapadesat \|\| 1.3.2 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because of the statement that He is attained by the liberated souls.**

**Комментарий:**

And because it is declared that He is attained by the liberated souls.

## \|\| 1.3.3 \|\|

*nanumanam atac-chabdat \|\| 1.3.3 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because no word describes prakrti as that abode.**

**Комментарий:**

That abode cannot be prakrti because in this passage of the Upanisad it is not seen that any word expresses it.

## \|\| 1.3.4 \|\|

*prana-bhrc ca \|\| 1.3.4 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because it also cannot be the living entity.**

**Комментарий:**

The living entity also cannot be that abode because there is no word expressing minuteness, and because the jiva is not endowed with attributes such as omniscience.

## \|\| 1.3.5 \|\|

*bheda-vyapadesac ca \|\| 1.3.5 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the difference between the Supreme Lord and the living entity is stated.**

**Комментарий:**

Because it is seen that the living entity and the Supreme Lord are different as the knower and the known.

## \|\| 1.3.6 \|\|

*prakaranat \|\| 1.3.6 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the topic here is the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

And because this section definitely deals with the Supreme Lord.

## \|\| 1.3.7 \|\|

*sthity-adanabhyam ca \|\| 1.3.7 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because of the description of two birds - one standing still and the other eating.**

**Комментарий:**

In a subsequent passage, it is seen that one bird, happily sitting, is not eating, and the bird eating is afflicted by grief. Out of the two, the first is the Supreme Lord. In the beginning passage, the Lord is to be taken as the abode of heaven, earth and so on. If taken otherwise, then His sudden description subsequently would be inappropriate.

## \|\| 1.3.8 \|\|

*bhuma samprasadad adhy upadesat \|\| 1.3.8 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Bhuma is the Supreme Lord, for He is declared to be higher than the living entity.**

**Комментарий:**

Sanat-kumara instructed Narada Muni about Bhuma (the great one). He is Narayana, the Supreme Lord, Who is declared to be superior to the living entity.

## \|\| 1.3.9 \|\|

*dharmopapattes ca \|\| 1.3.9 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because this is made clear by the described attributes.**

**Комментарий:**

One who attains Bhuma has nothing other than happiness and will never see death. By the evidence of this and other attributes, it is concluded that Bhuma is the Supreme Lord.

## \|\| 1.3.10 \|\|

*aksaram ambaranta-dhrteh \|\| 1.3.10 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is the imperishable (aksara), for He is supporting everything up to the sky.**

**Комментарий:**

The imperishable (aksara) is to be known as the Supreme Lord, for He is supporting everything up to the sky.

## \|\| 1.3.11 \|\|

*sa ca prasasanat \|\| 1.3.11 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And such support is possible because of His command.**

**Комментарий:**

Only a conscious being can perform such a support, for it is seen in the sruti that He commands.

## \|\| 1.3.12 \|\|

*anya-bhava-vyavrttes ca \|\| 1.3.12 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because pradhana is excluded, since it has a different nature.**

**Комментарий:**

I do not consider pradhana to be referred to here because of the impossibility of it being able to command. It is also excluded because it has a nature different than that of one able to see, etc.

## \|\| 1.3.13 \|\|

*iksati-karma-vyapadesat sah \|\| 1.3.13 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is the person meant, for He is expressed as the object of seeing.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord Visnu is the object of the verb ‘iksate ’ (one sees) and is heard of in the meditation on the pranava. That cannot be Lord Brahma, for it is seen that Lord Visnu has qualities such as being peaceful, undecaying, and so on.

## \|\| 1.3.14 \|\|

*dahara uttarebhyah \|\| 1.3.14 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is dahara, for this is understood from the subsequent statements.**

**Комментарий:**

From the remaining sentences, it is ascertained that ‘dahara’ (the small interspace) is Lord Visnu because His all-pervasiveness is understood from His similarity with ether.

## \|\| 1.3.15 \|\|

*gati-sabdabhyam tatha hi drstam lingam ca \|\| 1.3.15 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because of the words ‘Brahma-Ioka’ and ‘gati’ (destination), which express a characteristic of the Supreme Lord, as it is also seen in other texts.**

**Комментарий:**

To be the daily destination of the living entities is an indication of the Supreme Lord. The words ‘brahma-loka’ in the same sentence also express the same meaning. It is clearly seen in other sruti passages that the Supreme Lord is the daily destination of the living entities, and this is therefore seen as a characteristic of His. If ‘Brahma-loka’ is interpreted as Lord Brahma’s planet, then how could it be a daily destination ?

## \|\| 1.3.16 \|\|

*dhrtes ca mahimno’syasminn upalabdheh \|\| 1.3.16 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because it is understood that the glory for supporting this universe belongs to the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Because He is supporting all the worlds like a bridge, ‘dahara’ is ascertained to be the Supreme Lord. In other places it is also understood that the glory for supporting everything belongs to Lord Visnu.

## \|\| 1.3.17 \|\|

*prasiddhes ca \|\| 1.3.17 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the Supreme Lord is well-known as akasa.**

**Комментарий:**

Because in other places the word ‘akasa ’ (sky) is well-known to mean the Supreme Soul.

## \|\| 1.3.18 \|\|

*itara-paramarsat sa iti cen nasambhavat \|\| 1.3.18 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that dahara is the living entity because of its reference there, we say that this is not possible.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If by the remaining sentences "dahara ’ is said to be the Supreme Lord, then how it cannot be the living entity, since therein there is also a reference to the jiva?” It is not so, for qualities such as freedom from all sins cannot be possible in the case of the jiva.

## \|\| 1.3.19 \|\|

*uttarac ced avirbhuta-svarupas tu \|\| 1.3.19 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that dahara is understood as the living entity from the subsequent statements, we say that this is not true, for the qualities mentioned there pertain to one whose spiritual nature became manifest by worshipping the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “From the subsequent passage after Lord Brahma's statements it is seen that the living entity is dahara itself." It is not so, for the living entity is transmigrating in the material world, and the manifestation of its original spiritual nature is dependent on the worship of the Supreme Lord (dahara), Who, on the other hand, is eternally situated in His own nature.

## \|\| 1.3.20 \|\|

*anyarthas ca paramarsah \|\| 1.3.20 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The reference to the jiva has another purpose.**

**Комментарий:**

The reference to the living entity in that passage is regarded as meant to teach knowledge about the Supreme Lord.

## \|\| 1.3.21 \|\|

*alpa-sruter iti cet tad uktam \|\| 1.3.21 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that dahara is the jiva because the Upanisad describes it as small, we say that this has already been answered.**

**Комментарий:**

The confusion that the living entity might be 'dahara' because the Upanisad describes its minuteness has already been previously refuted by the aphorism that explains that the Lord occupies a small place for purpose of being meditated upon.

## \|\| 1.3.22 \|\|

*anukrtes tasya ca \|\| 1.3.22 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The living entity is not dahara, for it simply resembles the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Lord Hari makes everything become manifest and is manifest in everything. Because this well-known resemblance is observed in the sentence here, He is dahara.

## \|\| 1.3.23 \|\|

*api ca smaryate \|\| 1.3.23 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the smrtis also mention this resemblance.**

**Комментарий:**

And because such a conclusion is also seen in the Bhagavad-gita and other smrtis.

## \|\| 1.3.24 \|\|

*sabdad eva pramitah \|\| 1.3.24 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because of the word 'isana,' the Supreme Lord is the one of the size of a thumb.**

**Комментарий:**

On account of the word 'liana, ’ Lord Visnu is the one of the size of a thumb, and not the jiva.

## \|\| 1.3.25 \|\|

*hrdy apeksaya tu manusyadhikaratvat \|\| 1.3.25 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord is manifest in a size proportional to one’s heart, for the right to meditate belongs to human beings.**

**Комментарий:**

Although the Supreme Lord Sridhara is all-pervasive, by dint of His inconceivable energy He manifests Himself as having the size of a thumb according to the dimension of the heart of the meditator. Someone may argue, "If all living beings' hearts are equally considered to be of the size of a thumb, this judgement is not very sound. "Listen, although the Vedas are equally occupied with the welfare of all living beings, they give rights particularly to humans on account of their having qualities such as desire. Therefore, this rule is proved correct.

## \|\| 1.3.26 \|\|

*tad-upary api badarayanah sambhavat \|\| 1.3.26 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The same right is also possible for those in the upper planetary systems. So says Badarayana.**

**Комментарий:**

The heavenly denizens situated above the human abode have rights to study the scriptures, for they have desire, capability and detachment. Thus spoke Vyasadeva, the son of Satyavati.

## \|\| 1.3.27 \|\|

*virodhah karmaniti cen naneka-pratipatter darsanat \|\| 1.3.27 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that there would be a contradiction in the Vedic sacrifices, we say that this is not true, for it is seen that the demigods can assume multiple bodies.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If you consider that the demigods are confined within material bodies, then there will be a contradiction, for they cannot be simultaneously present in many Vedic sacrifices. ” It is not so, for on account of their yogic power, they can manifest many forms.

## \|\| 1.3.28 \|\|

*sabda iti cen natah prabhavat pratyaksanumanabhyam \|\| 1.3.28 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that there would be contradiction in the Vedic words, we say that this is not true, for according to the sruti and the smrti, the demigods are born from the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “There would be contradiction in the words of the Vedas, which are considered eternal, and therefore cannot be connected with the non-etemal bodies of the demigods. ” It is not so, for it is concluded from the sruti and the smrti that entities with real attributes, such the demigods, are born from the Vedas. There is no doubt about this.

## \|\| 1.3.29 \|\|

*ata eva ca nityatvam \|\| 1.3.29 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And therefore the Vedas are eternal.**

**Комментарий:**

Because whatever is expressed by the Vedas is eternal, the Vedic words expressing those things are also eternal.

## \|\| 1.3.30 \|\|

*samana-nama-rupatvac cavrttav apy avirodho darsanat smrtes ca \|\| 1.3.30 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because the same names and forms are again manifest when the creation is repeated, and because this is heard from the sruti and the smrti, there is no contradiction.**

**Комментарий:**

When created again, the whole universe, including the demigods, has always the same names and forms as at the time of destruction. Therefore, here there is no contradiction whatsoever, for this is heard both from the sruti and the smrti. In the Mahabharata it is described how Vyasadeva showed the dead heroes once again. Thus, the demigods are entitled to the scriptures.

## \|\| 1.3.31 \|\|

*madhv-adisv asambhavad anadhikaram jaiminih \|\| 1.3.31 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Jaimini says that the demigods are not entitled to meditations such as the Madhu-vidya because it is not possible for them.**

**Комментарий:**

Just as the demigods cannot be possibly entitled to engage in meditations such as the Madhu-vidya, not being able to be different than the object of worship, similarly, they are also not entitled to study the Vedas because they already have knowledge and are therefore indifferent. This is the statement of Jaimini.

## \|\| 1.3.32 \|\|

*jyotisi bhavac ca \|\| 1.3.32 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because they live in heaven.**

**Комментарий:**

Jaimini says that the demigods are not entitled because they are in connection with sensual objects and because they do not reside on the earth planet.

## \|\| 1.3.33 \|\|

*bhavam tu badarayano’sti hi \|\| 1.3.33 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**On account of scriptural evidence, Badarayana says that the demigods have rights to study the scriptures and practice certain meditations.**

**Комментарий:**

Badarayana holds the view that the demigods have the right to study the scriptures and practice certain meditations, for there are sruti statements to this effect. Jaimini's opinion is fallacious.

## \|\| 1.3.34 \|\|

*sug asya tad-anadara-sravanat tadadravanat sucyate hi \|\| 1.3.34 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is indicated that Janasruti was addressed as a ‘sudra' because he was lamenting after hearing the swans’ disregard for him, and then rushed to meet Raikva. Therefore, sudras are not entitled to study the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Although Raikva addressed Janasruti as a 'sudra' (one who laments), nevertheless he spoke the sruti to him. Therefore, here it is not stated that sudras are entitled to study the Vedas because they may desire to do so. By the narration of the swans, it is indicated that Janasruti was called ‘sudra’ because of lamenting.

## \|\| 1.3.35 \|\|

*ksatriyatvavagates cottaratra caitrarathena lihgat \|\| 1.3.35 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Janasruti is understood to be a ksatriya on account of the indications of royalty and the subsequent indication of a Caitraratha.**

**Комментарий:**

By sending a chamberlain, giving in charity, and other indications of royalty, as well as the indication of the connection with a Caitraratha king, it is understood that Janasruti was a ksatriya.

## \|\| 1.3.36 \|\|

*samskara-paramarsat tad-abhavabhilapac ca \|\| 1.3.36 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because samskaras must be taken into consideration and because of the statement that they lack them, sudras are not entitled to study the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

It is seen that samskaras must be taken into consideration for studying the Vedas. Since their lack of samskaras is mentioned, sudras are not entitled to study them.

## \|\| 1.3.37 \|\|

*tad-abhava-nirdharane ca pravrtteh \|\| 1.3.37 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because of Gautama’s conduct in ascertaining that Jabala was not a sudra.**

**Комментарий:**

On account of Jabala's truthfulness, Gautama Muni understood that he was not a Sudra, and having performed his samskara ceremony, he taught him the Vedas.

## \|\| 1.3.38 \|\|

*sravanadhyayanartha-pratisedhat smrtes ca \|\| 1.3.38 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the smrtis also prohibit Audras from studying the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

According to the smrtis, sudras are prohibited from hearing, reading and reciting the Vedas. Therefore, the conclusion is that they are not entitled to study them.

## \|\| 1.3.39 \|\|

*kampanat \|\| 1.3.39 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is called by words such as ‘thunderbolt,’ for He makes the world tremble.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord is ascertained from the scriptures by words like ‘vajra ’ (thunderbolt), ‘prana ’ (life-air), and so on, for these things are seen in the world as cause of trembling.

## \|\| 1.3.40 \|\|

*jyotir darsanat \|\| 1.3.40 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because it is seen that the Supreme Lord is also called ‘light.’**

**Комментарий:**

The word ‘jyotih’ (light) must be understood as the Supreme Lord, and not the orb of the sun. By analysing the previous and subsequent passages, it is concluded that ‘light’ means the Supreme Lord in this sentence.

## \|\| 1.3.41 \|\|

*akaso’rthantaratvadi-vyapadesat \|\| 1.3.41 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is called akasa due to the statement that He is different from name and form, being Their creator, and so on.**

**Комментарий:**

Only Lord Hari can be regarded by the word ‘akasa ’ here, never the sky, for His difference from it is seen by His being the creator of everything.

## \|\| 1.3.42 \|\|

*susupty-utkrantyor bhedena \|\| 1.3.42 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**That akasa cannot be the jiva, for in the states of deep sleep and when leaving the body there is a clear difference from the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The one described as knowledge personified is Lord Visnu, and not the living entity, for the difference between both is seen in the description of the state of deep sleep and the departure from the body.

## \|\| 1.3.43 \|\|

*paty-adi-sabdebhyah \|\| 1.3.43 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because this difference is understood from words such as 'pati' (Lord).**

**Комментарий:**

By words like ‘pati’ (Lord), it is concluded that the Supreme Lord is the one being referred to.

## Section 4

## \|\| 1.4.1 \|\|

*anumanikam apy ekesam iti cen na sarira-rupaka-vinyasta-grhiter darsayati ca \|\| 1.4.1 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that in some recension of the Vedas the same pradhana propounded by the Samkhya philosophers is inferred, we say that this is not true, for it is understood that avyakta is figuratively described as the body in the form of a chariot, and the text shows this.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “In some recension of the Vedas the Samkhya elements - sense objects, mind, intelligence, soul, avyakta (pradhana), and purusa - are respectively seen and hence this philosophy is supported by the sruti. " It is not so. The word 'avyakta’ is understood here as "the body in the form of a chariot, which was presented in the beginning of this passage. Thus, the Samkhya interpretation is refuted.

## \|\| 1.4.2 \|\|

*suksmam tu tad-arhatvat \|\| 1.4.2 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The body referred to here is the subtle one, for this befits the word ‘avyakta. ’**

**Комментарий:**

The learned understand the subtle body as the seed of the gross body, which befits the word 'avyakta,' since there is no difference between the meanings of both words.

## \|\| 1.4.3 \|\|

*tad-adhinatvad arthavat \|\| 1.4.3 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Prakrti is fruitful on account of its dependence on the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Since here a subtle cause is understood, this is in conformity with the Samkhya theory. ” It is not so, for here prakrti is understood as dependent on the Supreme Lord.

## \|\| 1.4.4 \|\|

*jneyatvavacanac ca \|\| 1.4.4 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because in the Katha Upanisad it is not stated that this avyakta is knowable.**

**Комментарий:**

Here it is not stated that this ‘avyakta' is known in the same way as pradhana is in the Samkhya system, in which it is stated that pradhana is knowable. There is no such a statement here, therefore pradhana is not meant in this passage.

## \|\| 1.4.5 \|\|

*vadatiti cen na prajno hi prakaranat \|\| 1.4.5 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that the text says that this unmanifest (pradhana) is knowable, we say that this is not true, for the topic there is the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The sruti speaks about this avyakta when it states that it is devoid of sound and so on. "It is not so, for in that passage the topic is the Supreme Lord, Who is therefore understood as the Supreme Conscious Person superior to the individual soul.

## \|\| 1.4.6 \|\|

*trayanam eva caivam upanyasah prasnas ca \|\| 1.4.6 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In the Katha Upanisad there is only mention of a question regarding three things.**

**Комментарий:**

The topic in the Katha Upanisad is the answers to Naciketa's questions regarding three things - the attainment of the favour of his father, knowledge about the fire of heaven, and spiritual knowledge. Nothing about any other topic is seen there.

## \|\| 1.4.7 \|\|

*mahadvac ca \|\| 1.4.7 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Just as mahat originates from prakrti, prakrti originates from the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Just as mahat originates from prakrti, prakrti originates from the Supreme Lord and is therefore considered subordinate to Him.

## \|\| 1.4.8 \|\|

*camasavad avisesat \|\| 1.4.8 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Aja cannot be the prakrti of Samkhya, for there is nothing specifying it as such, just as in the case of the word 'camasa.'**

**Комментарий:**

Just as the word 'camasa’ (vessel), without any specification, is taken as a subordinate item in a sacrificial ceremony, similarly the pradhana mentioned in the ‘aja-mantra’ of the Svetasvatara Upanisad should be understood as a limb subordinate to the Supreme Lord.

## \|\| 1.4.9 \|\|

*jyotir-upakrama tu tatha hy adhiyata eke \|\| 1.4.9 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This aja is the material energy starting with fire, as some Vedic branches teach.**

**Комментарий:**

Since there is unity between cause and effect, my conclusion is that this aja is that same material energy which some Vedic branches teach beginning with fire.

## \|\| 1.4.10 \|\|

*kalpanopadesac ca madhv-adivad avirodhah \|\| 1.4.10 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**There is consistency in this interpretation, since prakrti is imagined as a she-goat for teaching purposes, just like the sun is compared with honey, and so on.**

**Комментарий:**

For the sake of easy comprehension, prakrti is imagined as a she-goat (aja), just as the sun is imagined as honey. Therefore, everything here is correct.

## \|\| 1.4.11 \|\|

*na samkhyopasangrahad api nana-bhavad atirekac ca \|\| 1.4.11 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Samkhya is not mentioned in the fruti, for despite the enumeration of the elements, they do not match the Samkhya description, and also because there are two extra elements.**

**Комментарий:**

Samkhya philosophy is not referred to in the sentence of the Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad about the five elements, for in this way there would be twentyseven elements, and not five groups of five.

## \|\| 1.4.12 \|\|

*pranadayo vakya-sesat \|\| 1.4.12 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**There are five elements there beginning with prana, which are known from the rest of the sentence.**

**Комментарий:**

According to Vyasadeva, the five elements beginning with prana are ascertained from the rest of the sentence.

## \|\| 1.4.13 \|\|

*jyotisaikesam asaty anne \|\| 1.4.13 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The absence of the word 'anna' in the Kanva recension is filled by the word 'jyoti'**

**Комментарий:**

In the Kanva recension of the text, the word ‘anna’ (food) is not present, but the number of five is completed by the word ‘jyoti’ (light).

## \|\| 1.4.14 \|\|

*karanatvena cakasadisu yatha vyapadistokteh \|\| 1.4.14 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because the Supreme Lord is similarly described in the Upanisads as the cause of the sky and so on.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The Supreme Lord has been previously described as the cause and identified as the sole origin of everything but this is not possible on account of the difference in the sequence of the effects produced." It is not so, for just as the Supreme Lord is described in this Upanisad as the cause of the sky and so on, He is similarly described in other Upanisads too, and even though there may be contradiction in the sequence of effects, there is no contradiction in the cause.

## \|\| 1.4.15 \|\|

*samakarsat \|\| 1.4.15 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is called asat because He draws Himself out from changeable material nature.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Words like ‘akasa’(sky) can be connected with the Supreme Lord, but how can the word 'asat' (non-existent) be employed in connection with Him Whose form is eternally existent?”. The answer is that because the Supreme Lord draws Himself out from the changes of material existence (sat), therefore the word 'asat' is appropriate. To remain a doer aloof from the changes of material nature is called 'samakarsa. ’

## \|\| 1.4.16 \|\|

*jagad-vacitvat \|\| 1.4.16 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is meant in the Kausitaki-brahmana Upanisad because the word 'karma' denotes the creation of the universe.**

**Комментарий:**

The object of knowledge that King Ajatasatru spoke to Balaki is the Supreme Person, for the word 'karma’ refers to the creation of the universe.

## \|\| 1.4.17 \|\|

*jiva-mukhya-prana-lingan neti cet tad vyakhyatam \|\| 1.4.17 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that this passage cannot refer to the Supreme Lord because the characteristics belong to the jiva or the chief life-air, we say that this is not true. This has already been explained.**

**Комментарий:**

The characteristics here that seem to belong to the living entity or the chief life-air are to be explained by the wise as previously.16

## \|\| 1.4.18 \|\|

*anyartham tu jaiminih prasna-vyakhyanabhyam api caivam eke \|\| 1.4.18 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Based on the questions and answers there, Jaimini says that the mention of the jiva has another purpose. The texts of the Vajasaneya recension also say the same.**

**Комментарий:**

Jaimini says that in this passage the discussion about the jiva is actually for the purpose of teaching about the Supreme Lord.

## \|\| 1.4.19 \|\|

*vakyanvayat \|\| 1.4.19 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because of the natural order of the sentences, the Supreme Lord is referred to in the Maitreyi-brahmana.**

**Комментарий:**

At first sight, it seems that the jiva is the topic in the Maitreyi-brahmana of the Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad, but by analysing the natural order of the sentences, the Supreme Lord should be known there.

## \|\| 1.4.20 \|\|

*pratijna-siddher lingam asmarathyah \|\| 1.4.20 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Asmarathya says that by the promise made in the Upanisad it is proved that the characteristics belong to the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

“By knowing the Supreme Lord, everything that is unknown becomes known ” - exclusive meditation is the characteristic proved by this promise. By this it is understood that although the sentence was made in connection with the jiva, it speaks about the Supreme Lord, for the characteristics there belong to Him. This is the opinion of Asmarathya.

## \|\| 1.4.21 \|\|

*utkramisyata evam-bhavad ity audulomih \|\| 1.4.21 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Audulomi says that the jiva becomes Brahman because such is its nature upon leaving the body and attaining liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

Audulomi says that Brahman is meant here because the soul leaving the body becomes Brahman in the liberated stage.

## \|\| 1.4.22 \|\|

*avasthiter iti kasakrtsnah \|\| 1.4.22 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Kasakrtsna says that the Supreme Soul is referred to here, for He dwells inside the jiva.**

**Комментарий:**

Although the jiva is mentioned here, this sentence of the wise Yajnavalkya does not discard its meaning in connection with the Supreme Lord, Who is situated within all living entities as Paramatma. This is the opinion spoken by Kasakrtsna.

## \|\| 1.4.23 \|\|

*prakrtis ca pratijna-drstantanuparodhat \|\| 1.4.23 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord is both the efficient and the material cause of the universe, for this is not opposed to the statements and illustrations in the scriptures.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord is both the efficient cause and the material cause of the universe. Otherwise, by knowing Him to be only one of these, the statements and illustrations of the scriptures would be opposed.

## \|\| 1.4.24 \|\|

*abhidhyopadesac ca \|\| 1.4.24 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because of the Supreme Lord’s will to become many is mentioned in the scriptures.**

**Комментарий:**

The desire to become many indicates that the Supreme Lord is also the material cause of the universe.

## \|\| 1.4.25 \|\|

*saksac cobhayamnanat \|\| 1.4.25 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because it is directly mentioned that the Supreme Lord is both the efficient and the material cause.**

**Комментарий:**

Because the Supreme Lord is directly mentioned as both the efficient and the material cause.

## \|\| 1.4.26 \|\|

*atma-krteh parinamat \|\| 1.4.26 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because the Supreme Lord is Himself the creator and transforms Himself into the universe.**

**Комментарий:**

This universe is said to be a form of the Supreme Lord because He turns Himself into it. Therefore, it is clear that He is also its material cause.

## \|\| 1.4.27 \|\|

*yonis ca hi giyate \|\| 1.4.27 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the Supreme Lord is described as the original source of everything.**

**Комментарий:**

The best knowers of the Vedas describe the Supreme Lord as the original source of all beings, and therefore also consider Him as the instrumental cause of the universe.

## \|\| 1.4.28 \|\|

*etena sarve vyakhyata vyakhyatah \|\| 1.4.28 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By the arguments explained in this chapter all opposing systems have been refuted.**

**Комментарий:**

By refuting the Samkhya philosophy, all other similar philosophical systems have been demolished.

## Chapter 2

## Section 1

## \|\| 2.1.1 \|\|

*smrty-anavakasa-dosa-prasanga iti cen nanya-smrty-anavakasa-dosa-prasangat \|\| 2.1.1 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that there would be fault in considering the Samkhya smrti meaningless, we say that this is not true, for its very acceptance would be a fault and would render many other smrtis meaningless.**

**Комментарий:**

The reconciliation of different scriptural passages was done in the previous chapter, and now in this chapter their non-contradiction is shown. Someone may argue, “In this way the Samkhya treatise composed by the learned Kapila has been rendered meaningless. It is certainly a fault to consider it purposeless. ” Please do not say so, for by doing as you expect, other smrtis in conformity with the Vedas, such as the Manu-samhita, would become meaningless, and then there would be a great fault.

## \|\| 2.1.2 \|\|

*itaresam canupalabdheh \|\| 2.1.2 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because such a Samkhya philosophy is not found in other scriptures.**

**Комментарий:**

In the scriptures spoken by authorities like Manu, we do not find this Samkhya system. Therefore, this Samkhya concocted by an ignorant person is not accepted by us as evidence.

## \|\| 2.1.3 \|\|

*etena yogah pratyuktah \|\| 2.1.3 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By this refutation, the Yoga system has also been refuted.**

**Комментарий:**

By this refutation of Samkhya, the Yoga treatise has also been refuted on account of its similar reasoning.

## \|\| 2.1.4 \|\|

*na vilaksanatvad asya tathatvam ca sabdat \|\| 2.1.4 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Someone may object, “This universe cannot have been created by the Supreme Lord, for both have different characteristics. We also hear from the scriptures that the universe is devoid of knowledge.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The Supreme Lord is knowledge and bliss personified, but the universe is perceived as dull matter. Since both have different characteristics, they cannot be related as cause and effect. It is heard from the scriptures that the universe is devoid of knowledge and is therefore considered dull matter. Due to their causal relation, gold and a golden earring are not perceived as different things. ”

## \|\| 2.1.5 \|\|

*abhimani-vyapadesas tu visesanugatibhyam \|\| 2.1.5 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“The demigods who control the material elements are the ones designated by their names, because there is difference, and because the elements are controlled by the demigods.”**

**Комментарий:**

“The literal meaning of the sruti in statements like ‘the earth spoke ’ is also not able to refute the concept that the universe is dull matter, for the elements are designated by such names (earth, etc.) along with the demigods who control them, because there is a clear difference between the enjoyers and the elements (the objects of enjoyment), and because it is heard from the scriptures that the demigods enter into the elements controlled by them. \*

## \|\| 2.1.6 \|\|

*drsyate tu \|\| 2.1.6 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is not so, for effects that are different from the cause are also seen.**

**Комментарий:**

If the above arguments are put forward, Sri Vyasadeva replies that examples are seen: just as you see dull matter like hairs and nails coming from a living person, the same must also be true in the topic under discussion.

If someone argues that the material cause of hairs and nails is the body, which is also dull matter, we say that if this were true, then hairs and nails would also grow from a dead body.

This is the fact here: there is no absolute similarity between cause and effect, for this is not really possible. According to the view that just a fraction of attributes is similar to the cause, existent categories become manifest in this world.

Asti (sat, existence), bhati (cit, knowledge), priya (ananda, bliss), rupa (form), and nama (name) - these are the five categories. The first three constitute the spiritual reality, and the last two constitute the material universe. The best of saintly kings says that the first three are real and the last two are false.

Actually, everything that emanates from the Supreme Lord is conscious. On the other hand, whatever category appears to be inert matter in this world is baseless. By means of knowledge, devotion, and so on, when that disappears, then only bliss is manifest.

## \|\| 2.1.7 \|\|

*asad iti cen na pratisedha-matratvat \|\| 2.1.7 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that in this case, name and form should be nonexistent, we say that this is not true, for here there is a mere negation of their misperception.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Name and form are factually seen in non-existent effects, so how do you deny them as false ? ” It is not so. This is what I say: One who speaks that something non-existent (asat) is factual (sat) is certainly contradicting himself There is nothing to be rejected in this denial, for this universe is called dull matter on the basis of mere words, just like the perception of a shell as silver.

This is the fact here: On the basis of twofold scriptural statements, I accept that this universe is twofold - conscious and unconscious. The former is spiritual, and its appearance is known as birth. The latter is unconscious, and is called false because it is characterized by six kinds of transformations. This is what the wise say.

## \|\| 2.1.8 \|\|

*apitau tadvat prasahgad asamanjasam \|\| 2.1.8 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Someone may object, “Then the Vedas would be incoherent for declaring that the universe would have the same attributes as the Supreme Lord at the time of dissolution.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If the universe is conscious, then the Vedas are incoherent for declaring its dissolution, as the dissolution of a conscious universe would not be possible. ”

## \|\| 2.1.9 \|\|

*na tu drstanta-bhavat \|\| 2.1.9 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is not so, for there is an illustration in response to this.**

**Комментарий:**

It is not so, for in response to this, the state of deep sleep is an illustration. We perceive that all miseries that exist in the waking state vanish during deep sleep.

## \|\| 2.1.10 \|\|

*sva-paksa-dosac ca \|\| 2.1.10 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because there is flaw in the opponent’s view.**

**Комментарий:**

There is flaw in the view of Samkhya and other schools that state that this universe appears from pradhana, which is devoid of sound and so on, while the universe is characterized by having sound and so on, and is therefore different than pradhana. Moreover, just consider how the universe goes into pradhana, which has a totally different nature. Your view is also flawed, for here there is also dissimilarity between cause and effect.

## \|\| 2.1.11 \|\|

*tarkapratisthanad api anyathanumeyam iti ced evam apy anirmoksa-prasangah \|\| 2.1.11 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that it should be conjectured otherwise, we say that even thus the consequence is that there would be no liberation, for logic is not conclusive.**

**Комментарий:**

Logic is not conclusive, for it can be refuted by those who are more intelligent. Even if my inference is not refutable, still on account of the flaws of logic, there cannot be liberation, for the Vedas say that the Supreme Lord is known by the Upanisads.

## \|\| 2.1.12 \|\|

*etena sistaparigraha api vyakhyatah \|\| 2.1.12 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By this same reasoning, the remaining philosophers who do not accept the Vedas have also been refuted.**

**Комментарий:**

By refuting the Sdrhkhya view, the remaining philosophers like the followers of Nyaya, who are fond of argument, have also been refuted by the same reasoning, for they hold great similarity.

## \|\| 2.1.13 \|\|

*bhoktrapatter avibhagas cet syal lokavat \|\| 2.1.13 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that if the undivided Supreme Lord is also the material cause, then the distinction as enjoyer and enjoyed would not be possible, we say that this is not true, for in this world there are also similar instances.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Since the Supreme Lord is undivided, it is not possible such a division as enjoyer and enjoyed which is seen in the world." It is not so, for although the ocean is undivided, there are different things within it, such as waves, foam, and so on.

## \|\| 2.1.14 \|\|

*tad-ananyatvam arambhana-sabdadibhyah \|\| 2.1.14 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The non-difference between the Supreme Lord and the universe is understood from the passage starting with 'vacarambhana."**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Just as we can see the mutual difference between the waves and foam and the ocean, similarly there is an internal difference between the Supreme Lord and the universe. \* It is not so. There is no difference between existing objects and the Supreme Lord, for the same identity is understood from the passage starting with the words like "vacarambhana" (mere words). The transformation considered to be different from the Supreme Lord is said to be mere words, for it is unreal like a shell perceived as silver. This is what the learned say.

## \|\| 2.1.15 \|\|

*bhave copalabdheh \|\| 2.1.15 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the perception of an effect is possible when there is a cause.**

**Комментарий:**

The concept of effect is possible when there is cause, and the concept of attribute is possible when there is something which possesses the attribute. Therefore, by the concept of both, the knowers of the Vedas understand that there is no difference between cause and effect.

## \|\| 2.1.16 \|\|

*sattvac cavarasya \|\| 2.1.16 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the subsequent effect previously existed in the form of cause.**

**Комментарий:**

The effect existed previously in the form of cause, and the attribute existed previously in the form of its possessor, and by this principle both are not two different things.

## \|\| 2.1.17 \|\|

*asad-vyapadesan neti cen na dharmantarenavakya-sesat \|\| 2.1.17 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that it is not so because the word ‘asat’ (nonexistent) is mentioned, we say that this is not true because from the rest of the sentence ‘asat’ is understood as a different attribute.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The sruti states that in the beginning there was only ‘asat, ’ therefore the effect must have been ‘asat’ in the beginning. How can that be a form of the eternally existing (sat) Supreme Lord ?” The rest of the sentence says that ‘asat’ existed, therefore it is something different than non-existence. You should understand that by the word ‘asat’ it is meant that the universe previously existed in a subtle form.

## \|\| 2.1.18 \|\|

*yukteh sabdantarac ca \|\| 2.1.18 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because of logical reasoning and a different scriptural passage.**

**Комментарий:**

Just as those who desire to make objects like a pot take clay, similarly the Supreme Lord applies Himself in order to take the form of the universe. Either something existent eternally exists, or something non-existent never exists. From this logical reasoning and from the word 'sat' seen in the Upanisad, the effect does exist in the cause.

## \|\| 2.1.19 \|\|

*patavac ca \|\| 2.1.19 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because it is like a cloth.**

**Комментарий:**

A cloth is not different either when it is spread or folded.

## \|\| 2.1.20 \|\|

*yatha ca pranadih \|\| 2.1.20 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And just like the airs in the body are not different.**

**Комментарий:**

Just like the life-air is not different, although it has different names according to its place, similarly even when manifest as an effect in the form of the universe, the Supreme Soul is non-different from it.

## \|\| 2.1.21 \|\|

*itara-vyapadesad dhitakaranadi-dosa-prasaktih \|\| 2.1.21 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**According to the statement of others, this view results in flaws such as the creation of a disadvantageous universe.**

**Комментарий:**

In this way, the flaws of considering that the creation is conscious have been presented and refuted. Now the flaws of considering that the creation is inert, as posed by others, will be refuted. Someone may argue, “By Vedic sentences like ‘tat-tvam asi, ’ it is declared that the jivas are the same as the Supreme Lord. By performing this creation for His own sake, the Supreme Lord did something unbeneficial to Himself. This sort of flaws would result from this concept. ”

## \|\| 2.1.22 \|\|

*adhikam tu bheda-nirdesat \|\| 2.1.22 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is not true. The Supreme Lord is greater than the jiva, for the difference between both is described in the scriptures.**

**Комментарий:**

It is not so, for the difference is shown in the scriptures - the Supreme Lord is greater than the jiva. It is understood that since consciousness is common to both, therefore non-difference is mentioned. Just like there is a clear difference between diamonds and other objects that are made of earth, similarly, although the Supreme Lord is non-different, still He expands Himself in diff erent forms such as the jivas and so on.

## \|\| 2.1.23 \|\|

*asmadivac ca tad-anupapattih \|\| 2.1.23 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Just like stones are fragmental portions of earth, the jivas are fragmental portions of the Supreme Lord, and therefore the objection is unsubstantiated.**

**Комментарий:**

This being established, the flaw presented by others does not occur here.

## \|\| 2.1.24 \|\|

*upasamhara-darsanan neti cen na ksiravad dhi \|\| 2.1.24 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that we see that a collection of ingredients is required to produce an effect, we say that this is not true, just as in the case of milk.**

**Комментарий:**

Having resolved the objection that the creation is inert, now the view that the creation is conscious is stated. Someone may argue, “It is not appropriate to say that the Supreme Lord assumes the form of the universe according to His own will, for it is seen that a collection of ingredients is required to produce an effect, hence the efficient cause is different than the material cause. "Please do not speak so, for on account of His inconceivable energy the Supreme Lord is able to maintain both forms upon producing the universe, just as milk transforms into curd.

## \|\| 2.1.25 \|\|

*devadivad api loke \|\| 2.1.25 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Just like the demigods and other such beings act in this world.**

**Комментарий:**

The demigods and other such beings themselves also produce effects in this world, therefore the same is not impossible regarding the Supreme Lord.

## \|\| 2.1.26 \|\|

*krtsna-prasaktir niravayavatva-sabda-kopo va \|\| 2.1.26 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Someone may object, “If the universe is a form of the Supreme Lord, this would either imply that the universe is His whole being or part of Him, which contradicts the scriptural passages that state that He has no parts.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If only one consciousness assumes the form of the universe, then tell me: is it a complete or partial manifestation ? In the first case, your system of worship in which there is difference between the worshipper and the worshipped is not sound; in the second case, it would contradict the sruti statement that the Supreme Soul has no parts. ”

## \|\| 2.1.27 \|\|

*brutes tu sabda-mulatvat \|\| 2.1.27 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is not true, for the evidence to this is the scriptures, since they are the only basis of knowledge about the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

It is not so, and I think in this way on the strength of the sruti. You too, O learned opponent, should think in this way: it is mentioned in the scriptures that the Supreme Lord is equally the ultimate abode of everything. Logic is of no avail in this matter.

## \|\| 2.1.28 \|\|

*atmani caivam vicitras ca hi \|\| 2.1.28 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the Supreme Lord manifests many varieties of energies within His own self.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord creates this manifold conscious universe within His own self, for He has many varieties of energies.

## \|\| 2.1.29 \|\|

*sva-paksa-dosac ca \|\| 2.1.29 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the flaw is indeed in the opponent’s theory.**

**Комментарий:**

The aforementioned flaws actually exist in your (opponent's) theory. In the first case, pradhana would be destroyed; and in the second case, its definition as being partless would be contradicted. Your logic has no scriptural basis to rely on.

## \|\| 2.1.30 \|\|

*sarvopeta ca tad-darsanat \|\| 2.1.30 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because it is seen in the scriptures that He is endowed with all energies.**

**Комментарий:**

The sruti demonstrates that the Supreme Lord is endowed with all energies, opulences, and so on.

## \|\| 2.1.31 \|\|

*vikaranatvan neti cet tad uktam \|\| 2.1.31 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that the Supreme Lord cannot be the creator because He has no senses, we say that this is not true. This has been previously explained.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Since the Supreme Lord has no hands, legs, and so on, He cannot be the creator of the world. ” This is a misconception and the solution has been shown before.

## \|\| 2.1.32 \|\|

*na-prayojanavattvat \|\| 2.1.32 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Someone may object, “The Supreme Lord cannot be the creator, for He has no purpose in it. ”**

**Комментарий:**

In order to further clarify the misconception that the creation is inert, an objection is again raised, “The Supreme Lord is self-satisfied, so what would be His gain in the creation of the world? Even a fool does not engage in any activity without gaining something. ”

## \|\| 2.1.33 \|\|

*lokavat tu lila-kaivalyam \|\| 2.1.33 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is not so, for the Supreme Lord creates as a mere pastime, just like common people.**

**Комментарий:**

Even though the Supreme Lord is self-satisfied, still He engages in sporting just as common people do, and His absolute nature is not contradicted by this. Therefore, even the liberation of the conditioned souls is regarded as secondary.

## \|\| 2.1.34 \|\|

*vaisamya-nairghrnye na sapeksatvat tatha hi darsayati \|\| 2.1.34 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In creating the world, the Supreme Lord is not partial or cruel, for the scriptures show that the Jivas attain different results according to their previous activities.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If the Supreme Lord is the creator of the universe, then there is partiality and cruelty in His creation, for it is seen in the creation that people are filled with either misery or happiness. Therefore, the cause of creation must be something else. ” It is not so, for there are different activities that yield a variety of different results, and the Supreme Lord rewards the living entities accordingly. Therefore, there is no fault. There are scriptural statements that corroborate this, such as ‘the Supreme Lord instigates one to perform pious deeds to go to heaven and sinful deeds to go to hell. ’

## \|\| 2.1.35 \|\|

*na karmavibhagad iti cen nanaditvat \|\| 2.1.35 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that there was no division of karma before creation, we say that this is not true, for karma is beginningless.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Before creation, there was only one Supreme Lord alone and no karma existed, so how could He take into consideration the activities of the jivas?” Please do not speak so, for karma is eternal and beginningless. Therefore, the results of karma are not produced by the Supreme Lord, Who simply instigates the jivas to perform karma and is thus not blamed with the fault of cruelty and partiality.

## \|\| 2.1.36\|\|

*upapadyate capy upalabhyate ca \|\| 2.1.36 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is proved correct and it is seen in the scriptures.**

**Комментарий:**

The transmigration of the individual souls is also beginningless, otherwise, it would be illogical. This is also seen in the scriptures.

## Section 2

## \|\| 2.2.1 \|\|

*racananupapattes ca nanumanam \|\| 2.2.1 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because of the impossibility of its creation, pradhana cannot be inferred as the cause of the universe.**

**Комментарий:**

In the previous section of this chapter, Vyasadeva clarified his own view. Now he will show the many faults in the views of others, such as Samkhya. Since it is impossible for it to create the universe, pradhana cannot be inferred as its cause. The construction of a palace done by an inert agent has never been seen.

## \|\| 2.2.2 \|\|

*pravrttes ca \|\| 2.2.2 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the activity requires a conscious being.**

**Комментарий:**

The activity required in order to accomplish the creation implies the decline of the state of equal balance in pradhana, which is not possible because in the Samkhya theory there is no conscious being directing it.

## \|\| 2.2.3 \|\|

*payo’mbuvac cet tatrapi \|\| 2.2.3 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that pradhana automatically changes just as milk turns into curd or like water adopts many flavours, we say that in these cases too there is a conscious being directing.**

**Комментарий:**

If someone argues that pradhana's activity is just like that of milk, which transforms itself even being devoid of consciousness, we say that it is not so, for the Supreme Lord is the one who causes the action. These are the words of the Vedas.

## \|\| 2.2.4 \|\|

*vyatirekanavasthites canapeksatvat \|\| 2.2.4 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because Sankhya claims that pradhana is independent, since there was nothing besides it before creation.**

**Комментарий:**

Before creation, there was nothing except pradhana that could activate or obstruct pradhana, therefore pradhana is independent and must be thus said to produce the universe. Yet, in this case, there should also be creation at the time of dissolution. Nor can one say that at the time of dissolution the unseen results of karma are not awakened, hence there is stillness instead of creation, for even at that time they can be awakened and produce effects.

## \|\| 2.2.5 \|\|

*anyatrabhavac ca na trnadivat \|\| 2.2.5 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It cannot be said that the universe is produced automatically just like grass turns into milk, for milk is also not present anywhere else besides cows and so on.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Just like grass automatically appears in the form of milk, in a similar way, pradhana turns into the universe." It is not so, for it is not seen that grass becomes milk when eaten by a bull. Therefore, the wise say that the cause is the connection with a particular individual and the principle that the Supreme Lord's will must be present.

## \|\| 2.2.6 \|\|

*abhyupagame’py arthabhavat \|\| 2.2.6 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Even if we accept prakrti’s independent activity, it cannot be the creator, since it has no purpose in this.**

**Комментарий:**

There is no gain at all in accepting that prakrti becomes active on its own. You accept that prakrti is engaged in providing either enjoyment or liberation for purusa, but since neither of these can be accomplished, such activity is purposeless.

## \|\| 2.2.7 \|\|

*purusasmavad iti cet tathapi \|\| 2.2.7 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If you say that prakrti acts just like a blind man moves with the help of a lame man, or like iron moves when near a magnet, still your view is faulty.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Just like a blind man standing near a lame man can move, and just as iron in proximity with a magnet visibly moves, similarly prakrti becomes active." It is not so. This illustration is faulty, for it defeats the theory that prakrti is independent, and thus it works against what you wish to prove.

## \|\| 2.2.8 \|\|

*arigitvanupapattes ca \|\| 2.2.8 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the unbalanced state of the modes of nature is not possible.**

**Комментарий:**

You say that by the prominence of a mode of nature there is mutual subordination of the other modes, but this is also not possible, for it would risk defeating your theory that in pradhana the modes are equally balanced. Purusa being indifferent towards pradhana, the creation you propose would never be possible.

## \|\| 2.2.9 \|\|

*anyathanumitau ca jha-sakti-viyogat \|\| 2.2.9 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Even if the modes are inferred to be otherwise, still they lack the power of a conscious being.**

**Комментарий:**

If you argue that considering their effects, the modes of nature must have many varieties of states, still this view is also not free from fault, for the modes lack the power of a conscious being.

## \|\| 2.2.10 \|\|

*vipratisedhac casamanjasam \|\| 2.2.10 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**On account of its contradictions, the Samkhya philosophy is absurd.**

**Комментарий:**

On account of its mutually contradictory statements, the philosophical conclusion of the followers of the Samkhya system is absurd.

## \|\| 2.2.11 \|\|

*mahad-dirghavad va hrasva-parimandalabhyam \|\| 2.2.11 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Just like larger atoms cannot be produced merely by a combination of single and double ones.**

**Комментарий:**

How the Nyaya and Vaisesika theory is briefly analysed. In this system, a single atom is called ‘parimandala, ’ and a double atom is called ‘hrasva. ’ From a combination of a single atom with another one, a double atom is produced; from a combination of three double atoms, a triple atom is produced, which is called mahat. This mode of explanation, however, does not bear scrutiny, for you have accepted that from a partless cause comes an effect with parts, while it is seen in this world that both cause and effect must have parts, just like threads and cloth, and so on.

## \|\| 2.2.12 \|\|

*ubhayathapi na karmatas tad-abhavah \|\| 2.2.12 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Neither by contact with the atoms nor by contact with the souls can their karma cause activity in the atoms, without which there is no creation.**

**Комментарий:**

The Vaisesika view is that the activity in the atoms is produced by the karma of the jivas, and then by atomic combination, creation gradually takes place. However, this is no possible. Such activity cannot be produced by the karma of the jivas in contact with the atoms, for karma rests on the souls and therefore cannot become connected with the atoms. Since it rests on the souls, their karma also cannot produce activity in the atoms. The activity in the atoms cannot be produced by such karma, which rests somewhere else. Conjoined inherence is also not possible, for neither the souls nor the atoms have parts. In this way, in either case, there is no activity among the atoms, without which the universe cannot appear.

## \|\| 2.2.13 \|\|

*samavayabhyupagamac ca samyad anavasthiteh \|\| 2.2.13 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because by accepting inherent connection and consequentially similar connections, there would be infinite regress.**

**Комментарий:**

There cannot be inherent connection (samavaya) of the atoms with double atoms, for there would be a similar relationship with the connected atoms. In this way, there would be another inherent connection and your proposition would thus result in regressus ad infinitum. If you argue that such a relation is the essential nature in this connection, then why have you not accepted this before? There is no further reasoning in this argument.

## \|\| 2.2.14 \|\|

*nityam eva ca bhavat \|\| 2.2.14 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If the atoms are eternal and always active, then the universe should also be eternal.**

**Комментарий:**

If the activity of the atoms is part of its essential nature, then creation should be eternal, otherwise it should never happen.

## \|\| 2.2.15 \|\|

*rupadimattvac ca viparyayo darsanat \|\| 2.2.15 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because you claim that the atoms have eternal qualities, such as colour, while the opposite is seen.**

**Комментарий:**

In your view, the atoms are considered to be eternally endowed with qualities like colour, but since we observe that objects having these qualities are destroyed, this concept of eternity is also destroyed.

## \|\| 2.2.16 \|\|

*ubhayatha ca dosat \|\| 2.2.16 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because in both ways there is fault.**

**Комментарий:**

If you propose that the atoms are devoid of attributes such as colour, then their by-products should also be so. Thus, since in both ways there is fault, this Nyaya and Vaisesika theory is incorrect.

## \|\| 2.2.17 \|\|

*samudaya ubhaya-hetuke’pi tad-apraptih \|\| 2.2.17 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Even if there is a conglomerate produced by twofold aggregates, still the universe could not be produced.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the many faults in the Buddhist theory of aggregates are shown. The Buddhist say that from the combination of four kinds of atoms arise the external conglomerate (bahya-samudaya) in the form of earth, oceans, etc., and that from the fivefold aggregates (skandhas), the internal conglomerate in the form of mind (citta) and its modifications (caitta) is produced. All elements are considered momentary. In response, we say that your twofold aggregate cannot be correct, for you reject the existence of an eternal conscious being who mixes the atoms.

## \|\| 2.2.18 \|\|

*uttarottara-pratyayatvad iti cen notpatti-matra-nimittatvat \|\| 2.2.18 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone says that the universe is produced because one element is the cause of the other, we say that this is not true, for they are only causes of their respective effects, and not of the aggregate.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “My theory is that nescience (avidya) and so on are the respective causes of the subsequent elements, and thus the aggregate becomes factual." It is not so, for you simply point out that this element is the cause of that and so on, but you do not point at a definite cause of the aggregate.

## \|\| 2.2.19 \|\|

*uttarotpade ca purva-nirodhat \|\| 2.2.19 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Such a causal relation is actually not possible because when the subsequent element is produced, the previous one is already destroyed.**

**Комментарий:**

Although it was previously assumed that the elements are merely causes of the subsequent ones, this is also incorrect, for a momentary cause is already destroyed when the effect is produced. It is observed that the cause must be uninterruptedly connected with the effect.

## \|\| 2.2.20 \|\|

*asati pratijnoparodho yaugapadyam anyatha \|\| 2.2.20 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If the cause is non-existent, it contradicts the proposed theory; if it is existent, cause and effect would exit simultaneously.**

**Комментарий:**

If you say that the effect is produced from a non-existent cause, then your previous statement that the creation is made by fourfold atoms is baffled; and if you say that the effect is produced from an existent cause, then cause and effect would continue to exist simultaneously, and by observing the subsequent existence of a cause in the presence of the effect your statement that everything is momentary is contradicted.

## \|\| 2.2.21 \|\|

*pratisamkhyapratisamkhya-nirodhapraptir avicchedat \|\| 2.2.21 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Neither destruction caused by a conscious agent nor destruction caused by a non-conscious agent can possibly happen, for existent objects are perceived as an unbroken flow of impressions.**

**Комментарий:**

Destruction of existent objects caused by a conscious agent is called ‘pratisamkhya-nirodha, 'and destruction caused by a non-conscious agent is called "apratisamkhya-nirodha. 'Both of them cannot possibly happen, for the continuous and uninterrupted flow of impressions that accompany an existent object are perceived as unbroken.

## \|\| 2.2.22 \|\|

*ubhayatha ca dosat \|\| 2.2.22 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And there cannot be liberation, for in both cases there is fault.**

**Комментарий:**

The destruction of avidya, etc., is said to be a conscious destruction, but this cannot be liberation, for in both cases it is faulty: If such a destruction happens on its own, then your prescription of spiritual discipline is purposeless; and if it is caused by knowledge, then the theory accepted by you that the cause is destroyed after producing an effect is defeated.

## \|\| 2.2.23 \|\|

*akase cavisesat \|\| 2.2.23 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Their theory that the sky is non-existent is also wrong, for the sky is similarly endowed with qualities as is the case with the other elements.**

**Комментарий:**

Your statement that the sky is non-existent is also wrong, for the sky is an element just like earth and the rest, which are similarly endowed with qualities.

## \|\| 2.2.24 \|\|

*anusmrtes ca \|\| 2.2.24 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because remembrance contradicts momentariness.**

**Комментарий:**

Because one can later remember only an object that was previously experienced, the theory that everything is momentary is thus smashed.

## \|\| 2.2.25 \|\|

*nasato’drstatvat \|\| 2.2.25 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Something non-existent cannot produce an effect, for this is never seen.**

**Комментарий:**

If we accept that the cause is momentary, then there cannot be the slightest effect. No effect is ever seen from something non-existent, such as from men's horns.

## \|\| 2.2.26 \|\|

*udasinanam api caivam siddhih \|\| 2.2.26 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Otherwise, in this way even indifferent celibates would have offspring.**

**Комментарий:**

If there can be an effect from a non-existent cause as accepted by you, then why indifferent celibates do not have sons and grandsons?

## \|\| 2.2.27 \|\|

*nabhava upalabdheh \|\| 2.2.27 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is not true that external things do not exist, for they can be perceived.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “According to Vijnanavada (Yogacara), there is nothing external, such as pots. Ordinary affairs are possible without external objects, just like in dreams. The attributes in the form of pots are to be sought in the concepts of these objects. ” It is not so. O dullard, since things such as pots can be perceived, to say that external things do not exist is unbecoming. If a rascal does not believe that one becomes free from thirst when drinking water, then how will anyone believe his words ?

## \|\| 2.2.28 \|\|

*vaidharmyac ca na svapnadivat \|\| 2.2.28 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The external reality is not like a dream or fantasy, for it has a different nature.**

**Комментарий:**

The illustration that dreams proportionate ordinary affairs is not possible, for their different nature is clearly perceive - in the dream state, things are inconsistent, while in the waking state things are consistent.

## \|\| 2.2.29 \|\|

*na bhavo’nupalabdheh \|\| 2.2.29 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since you think there are no external objects, the impressions cannot exist.**

**Комментарий:**

The stated theory that varieties of concepts come from impressions (vasana) is not possible, for if the external objects do not exist, then where would the impressions come from ?

## \|\| 2.2.30 \|\|

*ksanikatvac ca \|\| 2.2.30 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And also because you consider everything as momentary.**

**Комментарий:**

If you consider that the resting place of the impressions is the store of consciousness (alaya-vijnana), then you should understand that the theory of momentariness accepted by you is already defeated.

## \|\| 2.2.31 \|\|

*sarvathanupapattes ca \|\| 2.2.31 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because this theory is completely unsubstantiated.**

**Комментарий:**

Your theory that everything is momentary is completely unsubstantiated, therefore from now on, keep silent.

## \|\| 2.2.32 \|\|

*naikasminn asambhavat \|\| 2.2.32 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Different categories cannot exist in the same object, for this is impossible.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the Jain doctrine is refuted, according to which there are two kinds of substances - conscious (jiva) and unconscious (ajiva). The conscious ones are said to be of six kinds, to have parts, and to have the dimension of their material bodies. The unconscious ones are of six kinds, such as earth and so on. All of these are again sevenfold, defined in terms of the sevenfold predication (sapta-bhahgi-nydya). They consider that in a single object there is a simultaneous connection of both existence and non-existence. However, it is not so, for existence and nonexistence are not simultaneously possible in a single object.

## \|\| 2.2.33 \|\|

*evam catmakartsnyam \|\| 2.2.33 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is also similarly impossible that the soul becomes incomplete.**

**Комментарий:**

Because you have already accepted that the soul is of the size of the body, if by dint of its previous karma the soul of an elephant obtains the body of a mosquito, then it would not completely fit in it.

## \|\| 2.2.34 \|\|

*na ca paryayad apy avirodho vikaradibhyah \|\| 2.2.34 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Nor can you say that there is no contradiction because there is an arrangement by accepting and rejecting parts of the soul, for this would imply change and so on.**

**Комментарий:**

If you consider that there is an arrangement by obtaining and quitting parts, then the consequence would be change, non-permanence and destruction of the soul.

## \|\| 2.2.35 \|\|

*antyavasthites cobhaya-nityatvad avisesah \|\| 2.2.35 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is also not possible that only at the time of liberation the soul will have a permanent size, for its size is eternal and never different, both in the conditioned and liberated stages.**

**Комментарий:**

If you say that at the time of liberation the soul's size is eternal because it has no more material bodies, then this is what I say: Because there is no difference in size, the soul's dimension is the same in the beginning in the middle, and at the end of its conditioned life. By contradicting the souls' eternity in all stages, your consistency is defeated. In this way, the non-Vedic doctrines, which are postulated by sinful people, have been refuted.

## \|\| 2.2.36 \|\|

*patyur asamanjasyat \|\| 2.2.36 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Pasupata doctrine is not correct, for it is inconsistent.**

**Комментарий:**

After having established the Vedic Supreme Lord by logic, the present sutra is stated. In accepting the logical existence of the Supreme Lord, there are no faults such as those presented in the previous sutras. Yet it is not correct that Pasupati is the supreme cause, as proposed by you, for by this reasoning there would be flaws such as partiality, which were previously mentioned.

## \|\| 2.2.37 \|\|

*sambandhanupapattes ca \|\| 2.2.37 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because there cannot be connection between Pasupati and anything else.**

**Комментарий:**

In your philosophy, pradhana, jiva and Pasupati are similarly all-pervasive and without parts, hence there can be no connection between them.

## \|\| 2.2.38 \|\|

*adhisthananupapattes ca \|\| 2.2.38 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because prakrti cannot be Pasupati’s dominion.**

**Комментарий:**

By dint of mundane logic, they consider Pasupati a ruler like a king, but since neither of them has parts, prakrti cannot be Pasupati’s dominion.

## \|\| 2.2.39 \|\|

*karanavac cen na bhogadibhyah \|\| 2.2.39 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone says that the Lord rules over pradhana as if it were a sense organ, we say that this is not true, for He would have to experience pleasure and pain.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Just as the jiva rules over the senses, which are devoid of parts, similarly the Lord rules over pradhana." It is not so, for in this case, the Lord would be similarly subjected to experience happiness and distress.

## \|\| 2.2.40 \|\|

*antavattvam asarvajnata va \|\| 2.2.40 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If the Supreme Lord had material senses, He would be either perishable or ignorant.**

**Комментарий:**

If the Supreme Lord knows the extension of pradhana, of the jivas and of Himself, then on account of being limited, He would be non-etemal; and if He does not know, then He would be ignorant.

## \|\| 2.2.41 \|\|

*utpatty-asambhavat \|\| 2.2.41 \|\|*

**Перевод:**
