The sruti says that those who have attained either the personal or impersonal form of the Supreme Lord through bhakti or jnana remain eternally in supreme bliss and never return again to this ocean of suffering caused by repeated birth and death.

*srimad-rajadhirajanam ajnaya racitam maya \|*  
*vidyabhusana-samjnena karika-bhasyam asritam \|*  
*yady apy atra na vaicitri kacid asti tathapi te \|*  
*modisyante mudam yaj jna labhante bala-bhasite \|\|*

**By the order of the venerable King of kings, Sawai Jai Singh II, this versified commentary has been composed by me, Vidyabhusana. Although there is nothing wonderful here, still His Majesty will take pleasure in it just as the learned enjoy the words of a child.**

## Appendix

## Chapter 3. Section 3

In the previous section, devotion (bhakti) to the Supreme Lord, Whose form is transcendental, was mentioned. Now in this section, such devotion is described. Bhakti has the Supreme Lord, Who is non-different from His form, as its object, hence the relation of both as shelter and sheltered. Thus begins the third section of this chapter which, having sixty-eight sutras and thirty-three adhikaranas, discusses the transcendental qualities of the Supreme Lord. With five sutras that describe the Supreme Lord as knowable by all the Vedas, the first adhikarana begins with the following words to demonstrate that His qualities should be combined:

## \|\| 3.3.1 \|\|

*sarva-vedanta-pratyayam codanady-avisesat \|\| 3.3.1 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Only one kind of direct worship of the Supreme Lord is known in all Upanisads, for the injunctions and so on found in the Vedas are also unanimous about this.**

**Комментарий:**

It was previously explained that the Supreme Lord is eternity, knowledge and bliss personified. Now in this section, His transcendental qualities are briefly discussed. If someone argues that since there are different attributes of the Lord described in different Vedic branches, there should be different kinds of worship, we say that it is not so. In all Upanisads it is established that the Supreme Lord’s direct worship is only one, for the scriptural injunctions and so on are unanimous about this.

## \|\| 3.3.2 \|\|

*bhedan neti cen naikasyam api \|\| 3.3.2 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that because different attributes of the Supreme Lord are described in the scriptures there must be different kinds of worship, we say that this is not true, for difference is seen even within the same Vedic branch.**

**Комментарий:**

A different kind of worship is not desirable simply because there are some differences in the Supreme Lord’s attributes described in the scriptures, for such differences are seen even in the same Vedic branch.

## \|\| 3.3.3 \|\|

*svadhyayasya tathatvena hi samacare’dhikarac ca savavac ca tan-niyamah \|\| 3.3.3 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Rituals like the siro-vrata are limbs of Vedic study, and not of vidya, for this is actually described in the Samacara. The rule is similar to that of the Sava sacrifice, whose right of performance belongs only to those of the Atharva Veda branch.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Rituals such as the siro-vrata, which are seen in the vidya of the Atharva Veda branch, do not belong to other Vedic branches. Therefore, there is difference among the vidyas. ” It is not so. The siro-vrata ritual is a limb of Vedic study (svadhyaya), and not of vidya, for this is actually described in the Samacara text of that branch. Just like the Sava sacrifice is performed only by those of the Atharva Veda branch, and not by others, similarly rituals like the siro-vrata are also performed only by them, and never by others.

## \|\| 3.3.4 \|\|

*darsayati ca \|\| 3.3.4 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The sruti also teaches this.**

**Комментарий:**

The sruti also teaches only one vidya, for there is no difference between the vidya described in different texts.

## \|\| 3.3.5 \|\|

*upasamharo’rthabhedad vidhi-sesavat samane ca \|\| 3.3.5 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because there is no difference in the object, the combination of the Lord’s qualities is like the combination of subordinate injunctions when there is similarity.**

**Комментарий:**

If the vidya presented in many places is understood as being the same, then the Supreme Lord’s qualities should be combined with each other, for there is no difference in the object, just like various rituals mentioned in several texts as belonging to the same kind of fire sacrifice are combined in its performance.

## \|\| 3.3.6 \|\|

*anyathatvam sabdad iti cen navisesat \|\| 3.3.6 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“If someone objects that from the sruti it is understood that there is difference between the vidyas, we say that this is not true, for there is no difference at all.”**

**Комментарий:**

Another opponent says, “If someone argues that the Vajasaneya text states that prana is the performer of the Udgitha meditation, while the Chandogya states that prana is the Udgitha itself, and therefore the conclusion should be otherwise, we say that it is not so, for there is no difference in the rest of the narration, such as the fight between the demigods and the demons, etc. Thus, there is only one Prana-vidya.”

## \|\| 3.3.7 \|\|

*na va prakarana-bhedat parovariyastvadivat \|\| 3.3.7 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is not so. The vidyas described there are not the same, for they have a different topic, just as the Parovariyastva meditation also shares the same name, and so on.**

**Комментарий:**

On account of the difference in the beginning of the narrations, both vidyas are not the same. Nor can they be considered non-different because of having the same name, for although the Parovariyastva meditation (the highest of all) is also called Udgitha, there are differences between them.

## \|\| 3.3.8 \|\|

*samjnatas cet tad uktam asti tu tad api \|\| 3.3.8 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that both vidyas are the same because both have the same name, we say that this is not true and it has already been answered.**

**Комментарий:**

It should be understood that the argument that both vidyas are said to be one because both are named Udgitha has already been refuted by the previous sutra.

## \|\| 3.3.9 \|\|

*vyaptes ca samanjasam \|\| 3.3.9 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the syllable Om is universally present in all the Vedas, its adjective is appropriate.**

**Комментарий:**

As the words "Om ’ and "Udgitha ’ are in apposition in that passage of the sruti, four possible concepts can be established about their relation: false attribution (adhyasa), opposition (apavada), identity (aikya), and adjective (visesana). In the case of false attribution, opposition, and identity, there is possibility of fault. Therefore, adjective is taken as the right conclusion. If the word "Udgitha’ is an adjective of "Om, ’ then there is no objection. Otherwise, Om would be taken in its common sense as the sacred syllable universally present in all Vedas.

## \|\| 3.3.10 \|\|

*sarvabhedad anyatreme \|\| 3.3.10 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since there is non-difference in a vidya described in scriptures of all Vedic branches, its characteristics mentioned in a particular text should be combined with those mentioned in other texts.**

**Комментарий:**

The qualities of prana mentioned in texts like the Chandogya, such as being the best of all, should be combined with those mentioned in other texts such as the Kausitaki, for there is no difference in the Prana-vidya taught in all Vedic branches.

## \|\| 3.3.11 \|\|

*anandadayah pradhanasya \|\| 3.3.11 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord’s qualities such as bliss should be combined.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord's qualities such as bliss define His very nature and should be combined, since they are present in all His forms.

## \|\| 3.3.12 \|\|

*priya-sirastvady-apraptir upacayapacayau hi bhede \|\| 3.3.12 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Qualities like 'having joy as His head’ are not to be combined in all forms, for increase and decrease are possible if there is difference.**

**Комментарий:**

Qualities like "having joy as His head’ are not to be combined in all forms, for in this way there would be inconsistency in the mood of meditation. Yet by His inconceivable energy such an increase and decrease of joy can exist in one particular form of the Lord, which makes possible such a manifestation of difference, even though His form is free from difference. This is the conclusion.

## \|\| 3.3.13 \|\|

*itare tv artha-samanyat \|\| 3.3.13 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Other qualities should always be combined because they equally apply to all forms of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If you think that there is no difference in the nature of the Supreme Lord’s qualities, then why do you restrict their combination in some forms ?” This is what we say: The Supreme Lord’s qualities that are well-known in the descriptions of His nature are applied to all of His forms, but not other qualities besides those. Therefore, qualities like "having joy as His head’ must not be applied in all forms. Other qualities like bliss and so on are to be always combined, for they are commonly present in all forms of the Supreme Lord.

## \|\| 3.3.14 \|\|

*adhyanaya prayojanabhavat \|\| 3.3.14 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Such qualities are only meant for meditation, since there is no other purpose.**

**Комментарий:**

The conclusion is that qualities like "having joy as His head, ' which are described for the purpose of meditation on a particular form, are meant for meditation only on that form, and not on others. The description of qualities such as "having joy as His head' are meant to achieve perfection in meditation, and therefore they serve no other purpose.

## \|\| 3.3.15 \|\|

*atma-sabdac ca \|\| 3.3.15 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And because the word 'atma' is used in that passage.**

**Комментарий:**

There is no such restricted meaning there, for the word 'atma' is seen in that passage. Being meditated upon in this way, the Supreme Lord certainly does not become manifest as having wings and so on.

## \|\| 3.3.16 \|\|

*atma-grhitir itaravad uttarat \|\| 3.3.16 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The word 'atma' is understood as the Supreme Soul, just as in other scriptural passages, because of the subsequent sentence.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “From the beginning of this passage, the connection of ideas is about an individual soul inferior to Him. Thus, how can the word 'atma' there be ascertained as the Supreme Soul?” Listen: Despite such a connection of ideas, the word 'atma ’ is understood there in the sense of the Supreme Soul because this is directly understood from a subsequent sentence which is conclusive, and because this meaning is similarly seen in other scriptural passages.

## \|\| 3.3.17 \|\|

*anvayad iti cet syad avadharanat \|\| 3.3.17 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that because of the connection of ideas there, the word 'atma' means the jiva, we say that this is not true, for the Supreme Soul is ascertained from the very beginning of the text.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “As the connection of ideas was about an individual soul inferior to the Supreme Soul, how can you come to such a conclusion simply based on a subsequent sentence ?” It is not so, for the Supreme Lord is ascertained therefrom the very beginning of that section.

## \|\| 3.3.18 \|\|

*karyakhyanad apurvam \|\| 3.3.18 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since acamana is already mentioned in the scriptures as a common procedure, the new thing here is the meditation of water as the dress of prana.**

**Комментарий:**

Now something else is stated regarding the previously mentioned Prana-vidya. In the Chandogya and the Vajasaneya texts, rinsing the mouth with water (acamana) and the meditation of water as the dress of prana are mentioned. To take the first as a command in this passage of the sruti is a mistake, for since acamana is already mentioned in the smrti as a common procedure, here there is no injunction for it at all. Therefore, the new thing being prescribed (apurva-vidhi) here is the meditation of water as the dress of prana while doing acamana.

## \|\| 3.3.19 \|\|

*samana evam cabhedat \|\| 3.3.19 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In the same Vedic branch, the vidyas taught are similarly only one, for there is no difference in the object of meditation.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The Sandilya-vidya described in the Vajasaneya branch is divided into two kinds, for in one of them there are more qualities, such as supreme command (vasitva), etc. ” It is not so. Having the same descriptions, such as the mano-maya, that vidya is considered only one, for it is possible to include in it the additional qualities, such as supreme command (vasitva), etc.

## \|\| 3.3.20 \|\|

*sambandhad evam anyatrapi \|\| 3.3.20 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“The same is also true in other cases because of the connection between the names.”**

**Комментарий:**

An opponent may say, “It is seen that in the Satya-vidya described in the Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad (5.5.1-2) there are two names connected with two persons - ‘ahar’ (day) is connected with the person in the orb of the sun, and "aham ’ (I) is connected with the person in the eye. In this passage too there is only one vidya on account of the mutual connection between both names. Just as the oneness of the two descriptions of the Sandilya-vidya has been previously demonstrated by their application, similarly there must be oneness in the two kinds of Satya-vidyas on account of the connection between the names.”

## \|\| 3.3.21 \|\|

*nava visesat \|\| 3.3.21 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is not so, for there is difference.**

## \|\| 3.3.22 \|\|

*darsayati ca \|\| 3.3.22 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The sruti also shows the difference.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the conclusion is stated. The sruti shows the difference: oneness of vidyas is not possible here because of the difference between the places of meditation - the orb of the sun and the eyes.

## \|\| 3.3.23 \|\|

*sambhrti-dyu-vyapty api catah \|\| 3.3.23 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Qualities like ‘being the supporter’ and ‘pervading the sky’ are also not combined for the same reason.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord’s qualities like "being the supporter’ and "pervading the sky’ are stated by the Ranayaniya texts in their supplementary portions. They are never combined with the Sandilya-vidya taught in the Vedic texts belonging to that branch, for there is a difference in the places of meditation.

## \|\| 3.3.24 \|\|

*purusa-vidyayam iva cetaresam anamnanat \|\| 3.3.24 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is similarly true in the Purusa-vidya, for its characteristics are not mentioned in other texts.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The Purusa-vidya described by the Tandi and the Taittiriya texts is only one, for it has similar statements; such as the comparison of death with the sacrificial bath. "It is not so. The characteristics mentioned in the Purusa-vidya in the Tandi text are not stated in the Taittiriya, and therefore both vidyas are different. When there is difference by many characteristics, equal identity is not desirable on the basis of a few characteristics.

## \|\| 3.3.25 \|\|

*vedhady artha-bhedat \|\| 3.3.25 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Qualities like piercing are not to be combined because they are part of a different subject matter.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Are qualities like piercing which are described in the texts of the Atharva Veda branch, etc., part of that vidya or not ? On the strength of their proximity with the description of the vidya, they must be.” It is not so. Being applied in violent activities, those qualities cannot be possibly included as part of meditations that have different subject matters, for indication (linga) and syntactical connection (vakya) are stronger than proximity (sthana).

## \|\| 3.3.26 \|\|

*hanau tupayana-sabda-sesatvat kusacchandah-stuty-upaganavat taduktam \|\| 3.3.26 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**When the wise person’s pious and sinful reactions are relinquished, they are accepted by others, for that sruti is complemented by another sruti that mentions their acceptance, just as stated about kusas, metres, praises and chants.**

**Комментарий:**

The relinquishment of pious and sinful reactions by the wise is described by the Tandi and Atharvanika texts, while the Satyayani texts declare that upon being given up by the wise, the pious reactions are accepted by dear ones like sons, and the sinful reactions are accepted by his enemies. This concept is clearly seen there. Because the sentence about the acceptance of reactions complements the statement about their relinquishment - when they are relinquished, they are accepted by others, and when they are accepted by others, they are relinquished - both are clearly stated in some places. A conclusion must be based on the peculiarity of what was spoken in a general way. Just like the sruti that merely states that the kusas are made from the wood of a tree is complemented by the sruti that states that they are made from the wood of the Udumbara tree, similarly when relinquishment is heard of, it is assumed that there must be acceptance, and when acceptance it is heard of, it is assumed that there must be relinquishment. Similar instances are seen in the case of metres, praises and chants.

## \|\| 3.3.27 \|\|

*samparaye tartavyabhavat tatha hy anye \|\| 3.3.27 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The pious and sinful reactions are relinquished at the time of death, for after that one has nothing to gain by them. So say the followers of another Vedic branch.**

**Комментарий:**

The relinquishment of pious and sinful reactions has been mentioned in the previous adhikarana. Does it happen before death or on the way to the spiritual world? Someone may argue, “It must be on the way because this is mentioned when crossing the River Viraja.” It is not so. The relinquishment of reactions is considered to happen at the time of death (samparaya), for after death such a liberated soul has nothing to gain by pious and sinful reactions. The followers of another Vedic branch say that he shakes off those reactions just like a horse shakes its hairs.

## \|\| 3.3.28 \|\|

*chandata ubhayavirodhat \|\| 3.3.28 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is the natural meaning, for there is no contradiction between both srutis.**

**Комментарий:**

The pious and sinful reactions must be removed before one attains the path of the demigods (devayana) - this is the meaning to be naturally conjectured in the statement of the Kausitaki-brahmana Upanisad. Both srutis teach that the pious and sinful reactions are removed before death, and because there is no contradiction between both sentences, this is the idea expressed there.

## \|\| 3.3.29 \|\|

*gater arthavattvam ubhayathanyatha hi virodhah \|\| 3.3.29 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The course traversed by the liberated soul is meaningful when considered as twofold, for otherwise there would be contradiction.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “At some places, the statement that the liberated soul goes through the path of the demigods is made in connection with the relinquishment of pious and sinful reactions, while in other places, it is not. Thus, there is a doubt whether the path of the demigods is to be taken in all cases or not. Just as one's reactions are to be taken by others, on the same principle, the path of the demigods must also be taken.” In answer to this, the following is stated: Such a course of the liberated soul can only be meaningful by being divided into two - the course of those who worship the Supreme Lord as having transcendental form and attributes (saguna), and the course of those who worship Him without form and attributes (nirguna). It would be a mistake to consider both as the same.

## \|\| 3.3.30 \|\|

*upapannas tal-laksanarthopalabdher lokavat \|\| 3.3.30 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is appropriate that only the saguna worshippers go through the path of the demigods, since it is seen that this is a means to attain a particular objective, just as in ordinary life.**

**Комментарий:**

To go through the path of the demigods is appropriate for the saguna worshippers, for in their case, it is seen that such a course is the means to attain an objective. Just as in ordinary life to reach a village it is required to go through a road, but to attain freedom from disease it is not required to go anywhere. Similarly only the saguna worshippers need to go through the path of the demigods, and not the nirguna worshippers.

## \|\| 3.3.31 \|\|

*aniyamah sarvasam avirodhah sabdanumanabhyam \|\| 3.3.31 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**There is no such rule that the path of the demigods is only part of the vidyas that mention it, for it is common to all of them. There is no contradiction about this in the sruti and the smrti.**

**Комментарий:**

“In the description of some kinds of saguna worship or meditation (vidyas) it is mentioned that the liberated soul goes through the path of the demigods, but not in other descriptions. On account of the description of this topic, the path of the demigods is part of the process only where it is mentioned. ” This is a mistaken idea. There is no such rule that the path of the demigods is only part of those vidyas which mention it. Rather, it is considered to be equally included in all kinds of saguna meditations, for there is no contradiction to this concept in the sruti and the smrti.

## \|\| 3.3.32 \|\|

*yavad-adhikaram avasthitir adhikarikanam \|\| 3.3.32 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Those in a position of authority remain in the universe so long as they have a duty.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The previous statement that the wise whose fruits of action have been fully destroyed before death have nothing to gain on the way to the spiritual world is not correct, for it is described in the scriptures that although Apantarama had attained knowledge, after giving up his body, he was bom as Vyasadeva.” It is not so. As long as such souls in a position of authority have a particular duty to undertake, so long they remain in the universe, and once their duty is fulfilled, they go to the Supreme Lord’s abode. They have nothing to gain by a material body. This is the conclusion.

## \|\| 3.3.33 \|\|

*aksara-dhiyam tv avarodhah samanya-tad-bhavabhyam aupasadavat tad uktam \|\| 3.3.33 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Concepts like being neither gross, nor minute, and so on, which are found in the imperishable Supreme Lord, should be combined, for they convey the same spiritual nature. This is said to be similar to the Upasad sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

Concepts like being neither gross, nor minute, nor short, and so on, which are found in the imperishable Supreme Lord, are to be combined in all forms without any doubt. Because such a negation of specific attributes is the same everywhere, the same Supreme Lord is known to be explained in this way.

## \|\| 3.3.34 \|\|

*iyad-amananat \|\| 3.3.34 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Although both are described in the same way in the rta-mantra, the individual soul and the Supreme Soul have different attributes.**

**Комментарий:**

It should be considered that there are two concepts expressed by the rta-mantra. Although in that mantra both the individual soul and the Supreme Soul are described as possessing the same attributes, drinking in connection with Lord Hari is considered similar to the logic of the umbrella carriers.

## \|\| 3.3.35 \|\|

*antara bhuta-gramavat svatmanah \|\| 3.3.35 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Soul is inside everyone’s heart, just as He is inside all material elements.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The Vajasaneya text beginning with 'yat saksat’ is stated twice, hence, on the basis of this repetition, there must be two different vidyas mentioned there. "It is not so. In both sentences there is only one vidya that describes the Supreme Soul inside everything that exists, as stated in the sruti 'sarva-bhutesu gudhah,’ etc. It is not possible that two all-pervading souls would be mentioned somewhere.

## \|\| 3.3.36 \|\|

*anyatha bhedanupapattir iti cen nopadesantaravat \|\| 3.3.36 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If someone objects that it is not so, for otherwise repetition in a different sentence would not be appropriate, we say that this is not true. Repetition is possible just as in the case of other instructions.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If the mentioned soul is considered the same, then such a repetition in a different sentence is not appropriate.” It is not so, for this is possible, just as in the case of sentences like ‘tat tvam asi.' Repetition of the same thing without a different reason is certainly well-known in common life.

## \|\| 3.3.37 \|\|

*vyatiharo visimsanti hitaravat \|\| 3.3.37 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The learned specify the interchanges of subject and object, for this is similarly seen in other passages.**

**Комментарий:**

In sentences like, “I am that, ” “That is me, " there are not two concepts, for the exchange of subject and object establishes their oneness in meaning. The learned specify both together in this way in order to demonstrate that both have the same meaning, just as it is well demonstrated in the sentence ‘tat tvam asi. ’

## \|\| 3.3.38 \|\|

*saiva hi satyadayah \|\| 3.3.38 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Satya-vidya is only one, for qualities like truth are part of it.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “What about the Satya-vidya, which is mentioned twice in the Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad? Is there difference in the vidya or not? In case of such a doubt, I say that on account of different results, the vidya must be different. ” It is not so. That Satya-vidya is considered to be only one, for the same context is drawn from the previous passage. Therefore, all qualities like truth and so on are accepted as part of that vidya.

## \|\| 3.3.39 \|\|

*kamaditaratra tatra cayatanadibhyah \|\| 3.3.39 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Qualities such as realizing all desires are to be combined because they share the same place of meditation and so on.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “In the Chandogya Upanisad, qualities such as realizing all desires are stated in connection with the small interspace within the heart, while in the Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad, qualities such as being the controller are mentioned in connection with the sky within the heart. Should the qualities of both vidyds be combined or not? From attributes such as ‘He is to be served, ’ and ‘He is to be known,’ there must be a different possessor of the attributes mentioned in both texts. Therefore, the qualities must not be combined. ” It is not so. Although there are differences in the vidya, these qualities should be combined, for there are similarities such as having the heart as the place of meditation, and because the object of knowledge is the same. The learned should know that the attributes such as being the controller, etc., described in connection with the small interspace within the heart, and the attributes such as realizing all desires, etc., described in connection with the sky within the heart, are all attributes of the same object of worship, the Supreme Soul situated there, and should therefore be combined.

*\|\| sri-sri-radha-govinda-tadiyarpanam astu \|\|*

## Baladeva Vidyabhusana Project

This publication is part of the Baladeva Vidyabhusana Project, whose main objectives are as follows:

1\. Search for lost manuscripts (such as the complete commentaries on the ten Upanisads and the Six Sandarbhas of Jiva Gosvami).

2\. Digitally preserve manuscripts currently existing in different libraries.

3\. Digitally preserve all editions of Vidyabhusana’s books.

4\. Digitally preserve articles written about Vidyabhusana.

5\. Type the texts in digital unicode, which can be converted into Devanagari, Bengali characters, etc.

6\. Prepare a critical edition of each work.

7\. Translate all the works into English.

8\. Publish all translations with the original Devanagari text.

9\. Investigate and verify existent biographical data on Vidyabhusana.

10\. Publish a comprehensive biographical work.

For more details, the readers may visit the following page:

www.vidyabhusanaproject.blogspot.com
